{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8d15f068-678c-4327-8e53-5715ca5c75dc","agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"Araştırma turu (salt-web; dosya DEĞİŞTİRME, git KOŞMA). GÖREV: https://www.quantconnect.com/learning (Boot Camp + kurslar) kataloğunu tara ve Meridian için DEĞER SÜZ.\n\nBAĞLAM: Meridian kendi motoru olan çalışan bir sistem — eğitim içeriğinin \"trading öğren\" katmanı bize değersiz. Değerli olabilecek üç şey: (1) PLATFORM-TEKNİĞİ dersleri — universe selection, custom data, ObjectStore, research→backtest köprüsü gibi QC-ölçüm iş akışımızı (EDG-021 tarzı kartlı ölçümler) hızlandıracak pratik reçeteler; (2) İYİ-KURULMUŞ metodoloji dersleri (varsa) — ör. overfitting/walk-forward anlatımları bizim standartlarımızla ÇELİŞİYOR mu örtüşüyor mu (çelişki de değerlidir, not düşülür); (3) sertifika/kurs yapısının OPERATÖRE kişisel değeri (kısa not).\n\nİŞ: katalog envanteri (kaç kurs/bootcamp, süreler, FREE mi) → her modülü üç kovaya ayır: platform-tekniği (bizim iş akışına maddesi ve URL'siyle), metodoloji (örtüşme/çelişki notuyla), alakasız (tek satır). Platform-tekniği kovasından çıkan SOMUT reçeteleri (ör. \"universe seçiminde X filtresi şöyle kurulur\") madde madde aktar — bunlar defter-v3 iyileştirmelerine girdi olur.\n\nDÖNÜŞ: yapılandırılmış rapor (envanter + üç kova + somut reçete listesi URL'li)."},"uuid":"dd637d3c-5f9a-41c2-b1b6-02ec6e9481fe","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:43:32.822Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

{"parentUuid":"939604d2-864a-4422-918d-4ea6b2a0ff5a","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- pdf-viewer:annotate: Collaboratively annotate a PDF — propose markup, review together, iterate\n- pdf-viewer:fill-form: Fill PDF form fields interactively with live visual feedback\n- pdf-viewer:open: Open a PDF in the interactive viewer\n- pdf-viewer:sign: Place a signature or initials image on a PDF\n- pdf-viewer:view-pdf: Interactive PDF viewer. Use when the user wants to open, show, or view a PDF and collaborate on it visually — annotate, highlight, stamp, fill form fields, place signature/initials, or review markup together. Not for summarization or text extraction (use native Read instead).\n- productivity:memory-management: Two-tier memory system that makes Claude a true workplace collaborator. Decodes shorthand, acronyms, nicknames, and internal language so Claude understands requests like a colleague would. CLAUDE.md for working memory, memory/ directory for the full knowledge base.\n- productivity:start: Initialize the productivity system and open the dashboard. Use when setting up the plugin for the first time, bootstrapping working memory from your existing task list, or decoding the shorthand (nicknames, acronyms, project codenames) you use in your todos.\n- productivity:task-management: Simple task management using a shared TASKS.md file. Reference this when the user asks about their tasks, wants to add/complete tasks, or needs help tracking commitments.\n- productivity:update: Sync tasks and refresh memory from your current activity. Use when pulling new assignments from your project tracker into TASKS.md, triaging stale or overdue tasks, filling memory gaps for unknown people or projects, or running a comprehensive scan to catch todos buried in chat and email.\n- cowork-plugin-management:cowork-plugin-customizer: Customize a Claude Code plugin for a specific organization's tools and workflows. Use when: customize plugin, set up plugin, configure plugin, tailor plugin, adjust plugin settings, customize plugin connectors, customize plugin skill, tweak plugin, modify plugin configuration.\n- cowork-plugin-management:create-cowork-plugin: Guide users through creating a new plugin from scratch in a cowork session. Use when users want to create a plugin, build a plugin, make a new plugin, develop a plugin, scaffold a plugin, start a plugin from scratch, or design a plugin. This skill requires Cowork mode with access to the outputs directory for delivering the final .plugin file.\n- enterprise-search:digest: Generate a daily or weekly digest of activity across all connected sources. Use when catching up after time away, starting the day and wanting a summary of mentions and action items, or reviewing a week's decisions and document updates grouped by project.\n- enterprise-search:knowledge-synthesis: Combines search results from multiple sources into coherent, deduplicated answers with source attribution. Handles confidence scoring based on freshness and authority, and summarizes large result sets effectively.\n- enterprise-search:search: Search across all connected sources in one query. Trigger with \"find that doc about...\", \"what did we decide on...\", \"where was the conversation about...\", or when looking for a decision, document, or discussion that could live in chat, email, cloud storage, or a project tracker.\n- enterprise-search:search-strategy: Query decomposition and multi-source search orchestration. Breaks natural language questions into targeted searches per source, translates queries into source-specific syntax, ranks results by relevance, and handles ambiguity and fallback strategies.\n- enterprise-search:source-management: Manages connected MCP sources for enterprise search. Detects available sources, guides users to connect new ones, handles source priority ordering, and manages rate limiting awareness.\n- design:accessibility-review: Run a WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility audit on a design or page. Trigger with \"audit accessibility\", \"check a11y\", \"is this accessible?\", or when reviewing a design for color contrast, keyboard navigation, touch target size, or screen reader behavior before handoff.\n- design:design-critique: Get structured design feedback on usability, hierarchy, and consistency. Trigger with \"review this design\", \"critique this mockup\", \"what do you think of this screen?\", or when sharing a Figma link or screenshot for feedback at any stage from exploration to final polish.\n- design:design-handoff: Generate developer handoff specs from a design. Use when a design is ready for engineering and needs a spec sheet covering layout, design tokens, component props, interaction states, responsive breakpoints, edge cases, and animation details.\n- design:design-system: Audit, document, or extend your design system. Use when checking for naming inconsistencies or hardcoded values across components, writing documentation for a component's variants, states, and accessibility notes, or designing a new pattern that fits the existing system.\n- design:research-synthesis: Synthesize user research into themes, insights, and recommendations. Use when you have interview transcripts, survey results, usability test notes, support tickets, or NPS responses that need to be distilled into patterns, user segments, and prioritized next steps.\n- design:user-research: Plan, conduct, and synthesize user research. Trigger with \"user research plan\", \"interview guide\", \"usability test\", \"survey design\", \"research questions\", or when the user needs help with any aspect of understanding their users through research.\n- design:ux-copy: Write or review UX copy — microcopy, error messages, empty states, CTAs. Trigger with \"write copy for\", \"what should this button say?\", \"review this error message\", or when naming a CTA, wording a confirmation dialog, filling an empty state, or writing onboarding text.\n- anthropic-skills:brand-guidelines: Applies Anthropic's official brand colors and typography to any sort of artifact that may benefit from having Anthropic's look-and-feel. Use it when brand colors or style guidelines, visual formatting, or company design standards apply.\n- anthropic-skills:canvas-design: Create beautiful visual art in .png and .pdf documents using design philosophy. You should use this skill when the user asks to create a poster, piece of art, design, or other static piece. Create original visual designs, never copying existing artists' work to avoid copyright violations.\n- anthropic-skills:consolidate-memory: Reflective pass over your memory files — merge duplicates, fix stale facts, prune the index.\n- anthropic-skills:contract-reviewer: Review any contract and flag what matters before you sign. Use this skill when the user says \"review this contract\", \"should I sign this\", \"check this agreement\", \"contract review\", \"what does this contract say\", \"flag anything bad\", \"review my lease\", \"NDA review\", \"freelance contract\", or pastes a contract, agreement, terms, or legal document they need reviewed before signing.\n- anthropic-skills:docx: Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx files) or Word templates (.dotx files). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, headings, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, performing find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file, use this skill. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or general coding tasks unrelated to document generation.\n- anthropic-skills:hpe-architecture-icons: Build HPE-branded architecture diagrams and use the official HPE technical icon library (273 icons × 8 color variants, extracted from the official HPE technical icon collection). Use this skill whenever the user wants an architecture diagram, network/infrastructure/topology drawing, solution schematic, or needs official HPE icons (server, storage, switch, firewall, cloud, backup...) for documents, slides, or HLDs. Trigger on \"architecture diagram\", \"mimari çizim\", \"mimari diyagram\", \"topoloji çizimi\", \"HPE icon\", \"teknik ikon\", \"network diagram\", \"altyapı şeması\", \"draw the solution\", \"çözümü çiz\", and whenever an HPE HLD/SOW needs a figure. Pairs with the hpe-word-branding skill (insert the rendered PNG/SVG into branded docs).\n- anthropic-skills:hpe-pptx-branding: Create HPE-branded PowerPoint presentations from the official HPE-PowerPoint-Standard-R9 templates (Light and Dark). Use this skill whenever the user wants an HPE deck, slides, presentation, pitch, executive summary deck, QBR, or wants to convert an HLD/SOW/document into slides. Trigger on \"HPE deck\", \"HPE sunum\", \"HPE slides\", \"sunum hazırla\", \"slayt\", \"executive summary deck\", \"yönetici sunumu\", \"pptx\", and any presentation task in an HPE context. Sibling of hpe-word-branding (same brand rules) and hpe-architecture-icons (diagrams/icons for slides).\n- anthropic-skills:hpe-word-branding: Apply HPE (Hewlett Packard Enterprise) corporate branding to Word documents (.docx). Use whenever the user wants to: create an HPE-branded Word doc from scratch or from Markdown, fix/rebrand/reformat an existing .docx to HPE brand standards, audit brand compliance, apply HPE fonts/colors/table styles, format RACI matrices, add a revision history, compare two versions ('what changed'), batch-brand a folder, convert/rebrand a PDF into an HPE Word document, or produce any professional Word doc following HPE guidelines (SOW, proposal, HLD, report, memo). Trigger on \"HPE branded\", \"HPE format\", \"make it HPE\", \"HPE Word doc\", \"HPE proposal\", \"HPE SOW\", \"HPE HLD\", \"rebrand this document\", \"convert this PDF to HPE\", and Turkish equivalents like \"HPE formatına çevir\", \"HPE şablonuna geçir\", \"HPE marka uyumu\", \"formatı HPE yap\", \"HPE formatında hazırla\", \"PDF'yi HPE formatına çevir\". Always use this skill even if the user just says \"fix the formatting\" / \"formatı düzelt\" on an HPE-related document.\n- anthropic-skills:morning: Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead.\n- anthropic-skills:pdf: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:pptx: Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. Not for backend-only or non-UI tasks.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Documentation QuantConnect.com\",\"url\":\"https://www.quantconnect.com/docs/v2/cloud-platform/learning-center\"},{\"title\":\"QuantConnect Boot Camp Tutorials\",\"url\":\"https://www.youtube.com/playlist?list=PLD7-B3LE6mz5jsEb127kdyJVMJrBNfbmI\"}]\n\nBased on the web search results, here's what I found about QuantConnect's Boot Camp:\n\n## Overview\n\nQuantConnect's Boot Camp is a coding lab designed to teach you the tools for quantitative trading, where you can improve your skills in finance, statistics, and software development and learn the QuantConnect API in easily digestible portions.\n\n## Course Structure\n\nBoot Camp consists of a series of Courses made of many Lessons involving many Tasks/Challenges, with tasks meant to be tiny and easy to achieve with hints and solutions available for you to learn. Each lesson builds on an understanding of the API from the lesson before it, so completing them in the order listed can feel intuitive.\n\n## Available Resources\n\nBoot Camp has a series of accompanying videos and quiz questions found as a Udemy course, with tutorial videos available on YouTube or in the Udemy course, and all the content is free.\n\n## Learning Path\n\nYou'll start out with the fundamentals for individual algorithm creation and move on to building an institutional-grade system using the Algorithm Framework, and will manage your portfolio, use indicators in technical trading strategies, trade on universes of assets, automate trades based on market behavior, and understand how data moves in and out of your algorithm.\n\nThe search results reference that there is a Boot Camp 101 / US Equities course available, but the complete detailed lesson-by-lesson list wasn't included in these search results. For the full catalog of individual lessons, you would need to visit QuantConnect's Learning Center directly.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"037a33d1-d5c8-407c-b177-a11141473507","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:43:50.882Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"c5591a59-3b66-47ea-9a2b-27747204b8fb","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

Guided strategy development in easily digestible portions.\n\nAuthor: QuantConnect\n\nFree\n | \n109,058 People Enrolled\n\n102 / FOREX\n\nLearn algorithmic trading with python for FX. Guided strategy development in easily digestible portions.\n\nAuthor: QuantConnect\n\nFree\n | \n23,680 People Enrolled\n\n103 / Futures\n\nLearn algorithmic trading with python for Futures. Guided strategy development in easily digestible portions.\n\nAuthor: QuantConnect\n\nFree\n | \n10,027 People Enrolled\n\n104 / Options\n\nLearn algorithmic trading with python for US Options. Strategy development in easily digestible portions.\n\nAuthor: QuantConnect\n\nFree\n | \n1,003 People Enrolled\n\nResearch Fundamentals Courses\n\nBuild essential research skills to test ideas and improve strategies.\n\nIntroduction to Data Analysis and Programming\n\nCourse 1/6: Learn the foundational tools for performing data analysis - the Research Environment, Python, Numpy, Pandas, and Matplotlib.\n\nAuthor: QuantConnect\n\nFree\n | \n2,409 People Enrolled\n\nStatistics and Basic Data Analysis\n\nCourse 2/6: Learn how to analyze and interpret the statistical moments, then explore correlations, random variables, and the instability of estimates.\n\nAuthor: QuantConnect\n\nFree\n | \n514 People Enrolled\n\nRegression Analysis\n\nCourse 3/6: Learn how to build regression models while respecting their underlying assumptions, how to quantify their fit, and how to avoid overfitting.\n\nAuthor: QuantConnect\n\nFree\n | \n299 People Enrolled\n\nStatistical Inference\n\nCourse 4/6: Learn about hypothesis testing, confidence intervals, and correlations in the context of algorithmic trading.\n\nAuthor: QuantConnect\n\nFree\n | \n153 People Enrolled\n\nFinancial and Risk Analysis\n\nCourse 5/6: Learn to evaluate and mitigate risks involved with trading, including concentration risks, factor exposures, slippage, market impact, and more.\n\nAuthor: QuantConnect\n\nFree\n | \n272 People Enrolled\n\nAdvanced Data Analysis and Algorithmic Trading\n\nCourse 6/6: Learn advanced trading topics that are essential to success, including PCA, autocorrelation, long-short portfolio formation, pairs trading, and Futures.\n\nAuthor: QuantConnect\n\nFree\n | \n334 People Enrolled\n\nThird-Party Courses\n\nExpand your knowledge with additional content from external experts.\n\nAlgorithmic Trading A-Z | The Complete Course\n\nIn this algorithmic trading tutorial series you will learn everything you need to know to start writing your own trading bots using Python and the QuantConnect quantitative trading platform.\n\nAuthor: Louis\n\nFree\n | \n34,322 People Enrolled\n\nComplete Algorithmic Trading Course\n\nMaster algorithmic trading with Python and AI. Backtest & live‑trade stocks, options, futures, forex and crypto\n\nAuthor: Cheng Li\n\nPaid\n | \nEnroll on Udemy\n\nPython for Finance and Algorithmic Trading with QuantConnect\n\nLearn to use Python, Pandas, Matplotlib, and the QuantConnect Lean Engine to perform financial analysis and trading.\n\nAuthor: Jose Portilla, Pierian Training\n\nPaid\n | \nEnroll on Udemy\n\nCrypto Trading with QuantConnect (C#)\n\nLearn to write programs that algorithmically trade cryptocurrencies using QuantConnect (C#).\n\nAuthor: Eric Summers\n\nPaid\n | \nEnroll on Udemy"},{"type":"text","text":"\n\nTab Context:\n- Executed on tabId: tab-2\n- Available tabs:\n  • tabId tab-2: \"Algorithmic Trading Platform - QuantConnect.com\" (https://quantconnect.com)"}]}]},"uuid":"313dd7ef-ba2f-4338-9924-987285ae7e97","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:47:25.668Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"cee7845f-1611-4fed-8c34-b83cf68c4941","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

{"parentUuid":"67687704-d1c0-4d0c-85b2-bb983d64d18a","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bfeb96d1-a925-4b27-ab67-9fd7eeb5bd85","agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FYkqHccUssM6oANVzJKtCs","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Title: Algorithmic Trading Platform - QuantConnect.com\nURL: https://quantconnect.com\nSource element: <body>\n---\nToggle navigation\n\nErdem Özt&uu...\nProjects\nFat Apricot Koala\nOrganization\nLearning\nDatasets\nStrategies\nLive\nSupport\nLearning Center\nAll Courses\nIn Progress\nCompleted\nWish List\nArticles\n101 / US Equities\n\nLearn algorithmic trading with python for US Equities. Guided strategy development in easily digestible portions.\n\nFree  |   109,058 People Enrolled  |  Author: QuantConnect\n\nEnroll Add to Wish List\n\n \n\nAbout Instructors Requirements Syllabus Reviews\n\nDescription\n\nIn this course, we give you an introduction to the basics of algorithmic trading on QuantConnect using US Equities.\n\nInstructors\nJared Broad\n\nQuantitative Developer\n\nJared is a New Zealand Biomedical Engineer, and the CEO and Founder of QuantConnect. Based in Seattle, Jared has worked in humanitarian work, medical technology, and for the last 10 years in quantitative finance.\nSherry Yang\n\nDeveloper Advocate\n\nSherry is a developer advocate at QuantConnect helping the community education initiatives.\nRequirements\n\nSolid knowledge of python is required to get the most value from the Boot Camp Course Series.\n\nSyllabus\n\nThe 101 / US Equities course covers 12 lessons with coding and video components. It is classified as Beginner difficulty.\n\nLESSON 1\n6 Tasks\n  |  \nVideo Included\n  |  \nInteractive Learning\n\nBuy and Hold / Equities\n\nLearn how to initialize an algorithm's backtesting date range, and starting portfolio value. You'll be able to request US Equity data and configure the resolution of the data. We'll explore two different normalization techniques; raw and normalized for adjusting the data delivered to your algorithm.\n\nStart Lesson\n\nLESSON 2\n5 Tasks\n  |  \nVideo Included\n  |  \nInteractive Learning\n\nBuy and Hold with a Trailing Stop\n\nIn this lesson we'll explore how to use a trailing stop to control the risk of an open position, and plot the price of your stops to visualize it triggering. You'll learn how to use the order event system to track when the order is triggered.\n\nLEAN supports trailing stop orders, but your brokerage may not. To check if your brokerage supports these orders, see the Orders section of the documentation for your brokerage model. If your brokerage doesn't support them, you can implement a workaround using just stop market orders.\n\nStart Lesson\n\nLESSON 3\n5 Tasks\n  |  \nVideo Included\n  |  \nInteractive Learning\n\nMomentum-Based Tactical Allocation\n\nIn this lesson, we'll track the momentum of a portfolio of assets and allocates capital to the best performers, using the SetHoldings method. We'll control the trading frequency to reduce the fees, and learn how to warm up an indicator.\n\nStart Lesson\n\nLESSON 4\n4 Tasks\n  |  \nVideo Included\n  |  \nInteractive Learning\n\nOpening Range Breakout\n\nIn this lesson, we'll build a strategy searching for price break outs from the first few minutes of trading using consolidators to create custom bars and use a scheduled event to exit the position.\n\nStart Lesson\n\nLESSON 5\n5 Tasks\n  |  \nVideo Included\n  |  \nInteractive Learning\n\nLiquid Universe Selection\n\nIn this lesson, we will focus on investing in the eight most liquid symbols from a collection of 8,000 tickers provided daily by QuantConnect. We will manage which assets are included in the algorithm by utilizing universe selection properties.\n\nThe video for this lesson covers the deprecated Legacy Universe. Instead, please use the modern unified version discussed in this lesson.\n\nStart Lesson\n\nLESSON 6\n4 Tasks\n  |  \nVideo Included\n  |  \nInteractive Learning\n\nFading The Gap\n\nIn this lesson, we bet against unusual overnight gaps in price in TSLA assuming the market will mean-reverse. We'll use a standard deviation indicator to remove a parameter from our strategy.\n\nStart Lesson\n\nLESSON 7\n4 Tasks\n  |  \nVideo Included\n  |  \nInteractive Learning\n\n200-50 EMA Momentum Universe\n\nIn this lesson, we invest in a universe of assets where their 50-day moving average is greater than their 200-day moving average. We combine techniques for universe selection, history, and grouping data with classes.\n\nThe video for this lesson covers the deprecated Legacy Universe. Instead, please use the modern unified version discussed in this lesson.\n\nStart Lesson\n\nLESSON 8\n6 Tasks\n  |  \nVideo Included\n  |  \nInteractive Learning\n\nThe Algorithm Framework\n\nIn this lesson, we introduce the Algorithm Framework, a scaffold for building larger algorithms that separate code in a way its components can be reused. You'll learn about Universe Selection, Alpha Signals, Portfolio Construction, Risk Management, and Execution modules.\n\nStart Lesson\n\nLESSON 9\n5 Tasks\n  |  \nVideo Included\n  |  \nInteractive Learning\n\nPairs Trading with SMA\n\nIn this lesson, we'll create a basic pairs trading strategy using an SMA indicator. You'll learn how to create a synthetic asset and investing when the underlying prices diverge.\n\nStart Lesson\n\nLESSON 10\n4 Tasks\n  |  \nVideo Included\n  |  \nInteractive Learning\n\nLiquid Value Stocks\n\nIn this lesson we harness the Morning Star corporate fundamental data to select companies with high value according to their PE ratio and invest equally in the selected companies.\n\nThe video for this lesson covers the deprecated Legacy Universe. Instead, please use the modern unified version discussed in this lesson.\n\nStart Lesson\n\nLESSON 11\n4 Tasks\n  |  \nVideo Included\n  |  \nInteractive Learning\n\nTiingo Sentiment Analysis on Stocks\n\nIn this lesson, we request a news feed from Tiingo and calculate a sentiment score on the companies mentioned, and invest when the signals aggregate above a minimum level for investment.\n\nStart Lesson\n\nLESSON 12\n4 Tasks\n  |  \nVideo Included\n  |  \nInteractive Learning\n\nSector Weighted Portfolio Construction\n\nIn this lesson, we use Morning Star fundamental data to group stocks by their industry and assign capital equally to each industry, dividing it among the stocks in the universe. We'll use portfolio targets to rely on the execution models to fulfill the portfolio.\n\nThe video for this lesson covers the deprecated Legacy Universe. Instead, please use the modern unified version discussed in this lesson.\n\nStart Lesson\n\nAbout the Course\n\nLevel: Beginner\n\nLessons: 12\n\nFormat: Interactive Lessons\n\nDuration: 56 Tasks 11 Videos\n\nCourse Progress:\n0%\n\n\t\n\nQuantConnect\n\nQuant\n\nSelected group of pseudo-humans that can type without looking at the keyboard and use a super advanced neural network to transform coffee and orange juice into code.\n\nRate this Course\n\nUser Reviews\n\nWhat people are saying about this\n\nMost Recent\nHighest Rating\nLowest Rating\nAll\n5 stars\n4 stars\n3 stars\n2 stars\n1 star\n\nEduardo T\n\n5\n\nJun 15, 2026\n\nThe content is straight to the point and focuses on what actually matters: getting code to run in production. I really liked how the framework separates the alpha signals from the capital allocation, which makes it much easier to organize the bot's structure without messing up risk management. Practicing with alternative sentiment data and sector filtering really opens your mind to building realistic setups, the automated validator can be a bit annoying sometimes, blocking progress over minor syntax quirks, but the technical content itself is pure production-grade stuffexcellent for anyone serious about algorithmic trading\n\nFernando Costa Castanheira\n\n5\n\nMar 22, 2026\n\nGood course for who is starting with LEAN and QuantConnect. I already had programming in Python proficiency, but this quantitative finance concepts and flows are new to me. \n\nNow, I now need more knowledge about:\n\nBacktest metrics in QuantConnect\nOptions (will be my next course)\n\nDIMITRIOS KOLETTIS\n\n5\n\nMar 15, 2026\n\nGood work\n\nHao\n\n5\n\nFeb 21, 2026\n\nThis lesson is well-structured. \n\nArsen Labovich\n\n4\n\nAug 19, 2025\n\nIt is good for beginners, but web IDE has a lot of issues, sometimes I have been trying to build solution code, and it wasn't working.\n\nYou need to reset project, refresh page and do a lot of stuff to make even solution code be builded.\n\nTechnosight\n\n5\n\nJul 25, 2025\n\nUseful content, helpful videos. UI is not that perfect. It's good to have this boot camp along with the documentation.\n\nkaijun tang\n\n5\n\nJul 10, 2025\n\nReally good content.\n\nPaul R\n\n4\n\nJul 01, 2025\n\nThis went well for me, mostly because I have been experimenting with the code for a while now. It is worth the time.\n\nPatrick Sodré\n\n5\n\nDec 16, 2024\n\nThank you for putting this together! \n\n \n\nThis is a must-take course for anyone starting with QuantConnect! \n\n \n\nDon't skip it, even if you are an expert python programmer. \n\n \n\nYou will thank yourself later.\n\nSerhii\n\n5\n\nNov 27, 2024\n\nVery practical approach, trading ideas supported with Python implementation, with video support is perfect!\n\nMarry apple\n\n3\n\nSep 11, 2024\n\nneed update of the class material, this is a free class can't ask for too much, however I do spend quite something to debug class material.  especially for someone not quite familiar with this platform and class\n\nTom Nahme\n\n5\n\nAug 25, 2024\n\n.\n\nxperia xperia\n\n2\n\nJul 06, 2024\n\nconfusing and lacks emphasis on key concepts ,random spelling errors and outdated LEAN syntax made it extra confusing\n\nJason Russell\n\n4\n\nJun 02, 2024\n\nThe course provides a mix of introductory and advanced concepts. {"parentUuid":"d2436df5-f560-4646-a7bf-9f8e273bb028","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bfeb96d1-a925-4b27-ab67-9fd7eeb5bd85","agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Ev2HeXcxcP98DdcLP7YPTD","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"\"Syllabus Reviews\\nDescription\\nIn this course we give you an introduction to the basics of algorithmic trading on QuantConnect using US Equities.\\nInstructors\\nJared Broad\\nQuantitative Developer\\nJared is a New Zealand Biomedical Engineer, and the CEO and Founder of QuantConnect. Based in Seattle, Jared has worked in humanitarian work, medical technology, and for the last 10 years in quantitative finance.\\nRequirements\\nSolid knowledge of python is required to get the most value from the Boot Camp Course Series.\\nSyllabus\\nThe 102 / FOREX course covers 1 lesson with coding and video components. It is classified as Beginner difficulty.\\nLESSON 1\\n5 Tasks\\n  |  \\nVideo Included\\n  |  \\nInteractive Learning\\nBuy and Hold / Forex\\nIn this lesson, we request FOREX bid-ask data for your algorithm at a specific resolution, from one of the brokerages supported on QuantConnect. You'll set the brokerage model to have your backtests better model your FX brokerage. You'll place trades and round the orders to the lot size required by the brokerage (OANDA or FXCM).\\nStart Lesson\\n\"\n\n(captured at origin https://quantconnect.com)"},{"type":"text","text":"\n\nTab Context:\n- Executed on tabId: tab-2\n- Available tabs:\n  • tabId tab-2: \"Algorithmic Trading Platform - QuantConnect.com\" (https://quantconnect.com)"}]}]},"uuid":"bc91d11f-4950-4d3f-863c-2a5e9908b6e1","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:47:52.594Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d2436df5-f560-4646-a7bf-9f8e273bb028","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

{"parentUuid":"8330adfa-b7e1-447d-8514-d7988bec13cc","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bfeb96d1-a925-4b27-ab67-9fd7eeb5bd85","agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HmrxXjzYgaTzWmnWHEUXWy","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"\"### COURSE 1\\nL1 [Beginner] Buy and Hold / Equities (7 tasks) :: Introduction Video | Set Starting Cash | Set Date Range | Manually Selecting Data | Set Data Normalization Mode | Checking Holdings | Placing Orders\\n   desc: <p>Buy and hold may be the simplest algorithm possible yet covers many aspects of building an algorithm. In this lesson we walk through the steps of initializing an algorithm to run a buy and hold strategy along with som\\nL2 [Beginner] Buy and Hold with a Trailing Stop (6 tasks) :: Introduction Video | Setting up a Stop Market Order | Understanding Order Events | Identifying a Stop Loss Hit | Creating a Trailing Stop Loss | Visualizing the Stop Levels\\n   desc: <p>In this lesson, we will explore trailing stops and learn how to create a Stop Loss to manage risk in our orders. Additionally, we will plot the price of the order levels.</p>\\nL3 [Beginner] Momentum-Based Tactical Allocation (6 tasks) :: Introduction Video | Setting Up Tactical Asset Allocation | Using a Momentum Percentage Indicator | Preparing an Indicator for Testing | Using our Signal to Flip | Time Our Market Moves\\n   desc: <p>In this lesson we will shift capital between the S&amp;P500 to a Bond ETF. You will work with a portfolio with multiple assets and use a momentum indicator to implement a tactical asset allocation strategy.</p>\\nL4 [Beginner] Opening Range Breakout (5 tasks) :: Introduction Video | Creating a Consolidator | Bar Data and Bar Time | Using the Output of the Consolidator | Scheduling Events\\n   desc: <p>In this lesson we will implement an opening range breakout strategy. This strategy predicts a breakout if the price leaves the range of the first 30 minutes of trading.</p>\\nL5 [Beginner] Liquid Universe Selection (6 tasks) :: Introduction Video | Setting Up a Coarse Universe Filter  | Understanding Coarse Fundamental Objects | Tracking Security Changes | Building Our Portfolio | Customizing Universe Settings\\n   desc: Using a universe selection filter, we will invest in the top 8 stocks which are liquid and cost more than $10 per share. To do this we will use coarse universe selection features.\\nL6 [Beginner] Fading The Gap (5 tasks) :: Introduction Video | Creating Scheduled Events | Creating a Rolling Window | Accessing a Rolling Window | Reducing a Parameter\\n   desc: In this lesson we will use scheduled events to monitor for overnight price gaps in the market and trade on abnormal activity. Additionally, we will use a standard deviation indicator to reduce a parameter.\\nL7 [Beginner] 200-50 EMA Momentum Universe (5 tasks) :: Introduction Video | Laying Our Universe Foundation | Grouping Data with Classes | Applying Class In Universe | Preparing Indicators with History\\n   desc: In this lesson we will select a universe of assets where the 50-EMA is greater than the 200 EMA, create a custom class to hold symbol specific data, and use the history API to prepare our indicators.\\nL8 [Intermediate] The Algorithm Framework (7 tasks) :: Introduction Video | Framework Overview | Universe Selection Model | Alpha Model | Portfolio Construction Model | Risk Model | Execution Model\\n   desc: In this lesson we introduce the QC Algorithm Framework, the scaffolding for reusable module design. We walk through the theory, input, and output of each aspect of the framework. We focus on the creation of an Alpha Mode\\nL9 [Intermediate] Pairs Trading with SMA (6 tasks) :: Introduction Video | What is Pairs Trading? | Creating a Synthetic Asset | Monitoring for Price Deviance | Taking Positions | Warming Our Pair Spread\\n   desc: In this lesson we’ll use the Algorithm Framework to implement a pairs trading strategy for monitoring divergence for two assets, Pepsi and Coca-Cola.\\nL10 [Intermediate] Liquid Value Stocks (5 tasks) :: Introduction Video | Requesting a Fundamental Universe | Selecting Liquid Stocks | Accessing Fine Selection Properties | Using Fundamental Data to Emit Insights\\n   desc: In this lesson we'll filter a universe of stocks using fundamental data. We'll update our portfolio each month, buying the 10 best value stocks based on earnings yield, and shorting the worst value stocks.\\nL11 [Intermediate] Tiingo Sentiment Analysis on Stocks (5 tasks) :: Introduction Video | Introducing Alternative Data | Adding Alternative Data | Maintaining Algorithm Data | Managing Insight Emission\\n   desc: In this lesson we'll introduce alternative data and perform sentiment analysis with Tiingo News Data to trade Apple and Nike stock.\\nL12 [Intermediate] Sector Weighted Portfolio Construction (4 tasks) :: Selecting Universe By Sector | Sector Weighting Portfolio Construction | Tracking Security Changes | Calculating Target Weight Percentages\\n   desc: In this lesson, we create a sector balanced portfolio by implementing a Sector Weighting Portfolio Construction Model (SWPCM) with a universe of equities from the technology, financial services, and consumer goods sector\\n### COURSE 2\\nL1 [Beginner] Buy and Hold / Forex (5 tasks) :: Request Forex Data | Set Brokerage Model | Accessing Quote Prices | Applying Lot Size Rounding | Placing Forex Orders\\n   desc: <p>In this lesson we will implement a buy and hold strategy on FOREX assets - exploring how to request FOREX data; set your brokerage models, leverage and placing your first FOREX trades.</p>\\n### COURSE 3\\nL1 [Intermediate] Buy and Hold / Futures (5 tasks) :: Introduction Video | Request Futures Data | Navigating the Futures Contract Chain | Margin, Contract Multipliers, and Trading | Margin Calls\\n   desc: This lesson will use a buy and hold algorithm to cover many aspects of working with futures data. We'll sort and filter futures contracts by open interest, calculate our position size, place orders, and manage for margin\\n### COURSE 5\\nL1 [Beginner] Introduction (1 tasks) :: Introduction\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL2 [Beginner] The Algorithm Development Process (1 tasks) :: The Algorithm Development Process\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL3 [Beginner] Key Concepts (1 tasks) :: Key Concepts\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL4 [Beginner] Handling Data (1 tasks) :: Handling Data\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL5 [Beginner] Trading and Orders (1 tasks) :: Trading and Orders\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL6 [Beginner] Indicators and Historical Data (1 tasks) :: Indicators and Historical Data\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL7 [Beginner] Consolidators and Rolling Windows (1 tasks) :: Consolidators and Rolling Windows\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL8 [Beginner] Dynamic Universes (1 tasks) :: Dynamic Universes\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL9 [Beginner] Custom Data (1 tasks) :: Custom Data\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL10 [Beginner] Analyzing and Evaluating Results (1 tasks) :: First Task\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL11 [Beginner] Forex and CFD Trading (1 tasks) :: Forex Trading\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL12 [Beginner] Options Trading (1 tasks) :: Options Trading\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL13 [Beginner] OptionChainProvider Example Algorithm (1 tasks) :: OptionChainProvider Example Algorithm\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL14 [Beginner] The Algorithm Framework (1 tasks) :: The Algorithm Framework\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL15 [Beginner] Data-Driven Research (1 tasks) :: Data-Driven Research\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL16 [Beginner] Machine Learning Example (1 tasks) :: Machine Learning Example\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\\nL17 [Beginner] Deployment - Live Trading (1 tasks) :: Live Trading\\n   desc: Empty lesson description.\"\n\n(captured at origin https://quantconnect.com)"},{"type":"text","text":"\n\nTab Context:\n- Executed on tabId: tab-2\n- Available tabs:\n  • tabId tab-2: \"Algorithmic Trading Platform - QuantConnect.com\" (https://quantconnect.com)"}]}]},"uuid":"ac2b49ba-e2df-4b4c-98fe-878ed53577d1","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:48:41.088Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8330adfa-b7e1-447d-8514-d7988bec13cc","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

{"parentUuid":"8d0f69ad-1d26-4511-b7a4-c90424fe31b7","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"c0994d24-7ebc-4999-8662-834f71c61594","agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016WPKxuoCkv9H4QhBN9PkD4","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"\"L1 Buy and Hold / Equities\\n  LEARN: Learn how to initialize an algorithm's backtesting date range, and starting portfolio value. You'll be able to request US Equity data and configure the resolution of the data. We'll explore two different normalization techniques; raw and normalized for adjusting the data delivered to your algorithm.\\n  VIDEO: ; 0:00Set Starting Cash; 1:27Set Date Range; 2:08Manually Selecting Data; 3:35Set Data Normalization Mode; 5:04Checking Holdings; 6:07Placing Orders\\nL2 Buy and Hold with a Trailing Stop\\n  LEARN: In this lesson we'll explore how to use a trailing stop to control the risk of an open position, and plot the price of your stops to visualize it triggering. You'll learn how to use the order event system to track when the order is triggered. LEAN supports trailing stop orders , but your brokerage may not. To check if your brokerage supports these orders, see the Orders section of the documentation for your brokerage model . If your brokerage doesn't support them, you can implement a workaround using just stop market orders.\\n  VIDEO: ; 00:00Setting up a Stop Market Order; 02:41Understanding Order Events; 05:18Identifying a Stop Loss Hit; 10:01Creating a Trailing Stop Loss; 12:40Visualizing the Stop Levels\\nL3 Momentum-Based Tactical Allocation\\n  LEARN: In this lesson, we'll track the momentum of a portfolio of assets and allocates capital to the best performers, using the SetHoldings method. We'll control the trading frequency to reduce the fees, and learn how to warm up an indicator.\\n  VIDEO: ; 0:00Setting Up Tactical Allocation; 1:53Momentum Percentage Indicator; 3:45Preparing an Indicator for Testing; 5:33Using our Signal to Flip; 8:09Time Our Market Moves\\nL4 Opening Range Breakout\\n  LEARN: In this lesson, we'll build a strategy searching for price break outs from the first few minutes of trading using consolidators to create custom bars and use a scheduled event to exit the position.\\n  VIDEO: ; 0:00Creating a Consolidator; 3:36Bar Data and Bar Time; 5:51Output of the Consolidator; 8:20Scheduling Events\\nL5 Liquid Universe Selection\\n  LEARN: In this lesson, we will focus on investing in the eight most liquid symbols from a collection of 8,000 tickers provided daily by QuantConnect. We will manage which assets are included in the algorithm by utilizing universe selection properties. The video for this lesson covers the deprecated Legacy Universe . Instead, please use the modern unified version discussed in this lesson.\\n  VIDEO: ; 0:00Setting Up a Coarse Universe Filter; 2:37Coarse Fundamental Data; 5:15Tracking Security Changes; 6:31Building Our Portfolio; 8:20Customizing Universe Settings\\nL6 Fading The Gap\\n  LEARN: In this lesson, we bet against unusual overnight gaps in price in TSLA assuming the market will mean-reverse. We'll use a standard deviation indicator to remove a parameter from our strategy.\\n  VIDEO: ; 0:00Creating Scheduled Events; 3:40Creating a Rolling Window; 5:43Accessing a Rolling Window; 7:38Reducing a Parameter\\nL7 200-50 EMA Momentum Universe\\n  LEARN: In this lesson, we invest in a universe of assets where their 50-day moving average is greater than their 200-day moving average. We combine techniques for universe selection, history, and grouping data with classes. The video for this lesson covers the deprecated Legacy Universe . Instead, please use the modern unified version discussed in this lesson.\\n  VIDEO: ; 00:00Laying Our Universe Foundation; 03:22Grouping Data with Classes; 06:27Applying Class in Universe; 10:11Preparing Indicators with History\\nL8 The Algorithm Framework\\n  LEARN: In this lesson, we introduce the Algorithm Framework, a scaffold for building larger algorithms that separate code in a way its components can be reused. You'll learn about Universe Selection, Alpha Signals, Portfolio Construction, Risk Management, and Execution modules.\\n  VIDEO: ; 00:00Framework Overview; 02:46Universe Selection Model; 05:20Alpha Model; 10:39Portfolio Construction Model; 12:40Risk Model; 14:27Execution Model\\nL9 Pairs Trading with SMA\\n  LEARN: In this lesson, we'll create a basic pairs trading strategy using an SMA indicator. You'll learn how to create a synthetic asset and investing when the underlying prices diverge.\\n  VIDEO: ; 00:00What is Pairs Trading?; 03:53Creating a Synthetic Asset; 05:55Monitoring for Price Deviance; 08:45Taking Positions; 13:00Warming Our Pair Spread\\nL10 Liquid Value Stocks\\n  LEARN: In this lesson we harness the Morning Star corporate fundamental data to select companies with high value according to their PE ratio and invest equally in the selected companies. The video for this lesson covers the deprecated Legacy Universe . Instead, please use the modern unified version discussed in this lesson.\\n  VIDEO: ; 0:00Fundamental Universes; 2:10Selecting Liquid Stocks; 4:34Accessing Fine Selection Properties; 7:29Using Fundamental Data\\nL11 Tiingo Sentiment Analysis on Stocks\\n  LEARN: In this lesson, we request a news feed from Tiingo and calculate a sentiment score on the companies mentioned, and invest when the signals aggregate above a minimum level for investment.\\n  VIDEO: ; 00:00Introducing Alternative Data; 04:22Adding Alternative Data; 08:24Maintaining Algorithm Data; 11:45Managing Insight Emission\\nL12 Sector Weighted Portfolio Construction\\n  LEARN: In this lesson, we use Morning Star fundamental data to group stocks by their industry and assign capital equally to each industry, dividing it among the stocks in the universe. We'll use portfolio targets to rely on the execution models to fulfill the portfolio. The video for this lesson covers the deprecated Legacy Universe . Instead, please use the modern unified version discussed in this lesson.\\n  VIDEO: \"\n\n(captured at origin https://quantconnect.com)"},{"type":"text","text":"\n\nTab Context:\n- Executed on tabId: tab-2\n- Available tabs:\n  • tabId tab-2: \"Algorithmic Trading Platform - QuantConnect.com\" (https://quantconnect.com)"}]}]},"uuid":"c195ca24-00fe-45c3-b8d6-62505ef04221","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:49:29.258Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8d0f69ad-1d26-4511-b7a4-c90424fe31b7","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

{"parentUuid":"e3d98744-edba-409f-8309-03ca24b70eda","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"c0994d24-7ebc-4999-8662-834f71c61594","agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SWZhenFMED8y2SHudVR6Qv","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"navigated to https://quantconnect.com"},{"type":"text","text":"\n\nTab Context:\n- Executed on tabId: tab-2\n- Available tabs:\n  • tabId tab-2: \"Stock Selection Strategy Based On Fundamental Factors\" (https://quantconnect.com)"}]}]},"uuid":"7801a52c-a7ea-455c-910e-eaf8a64a20b7","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:49:58.605Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e3d98744-edba-409f-8309-03ca24b70eda","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"7801a52c-a7ea-455c-910e-eaf8a64a20b7","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdejpXW5UzaSmqLgJUsgF","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01GBG6vZgjgsppspEQY1xhUP","name":"mcp__Claude_Browser__javascript_tool","input":{"action":"javascript_exec","tabId":"tab-2","text":"(()=>{const t=document.body.innerText;return t.replace(/\\n{2,}/g,'\\n').slice(0,7000);})()"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":269,"cache_read_input_tokens":86672,"output_tokens":159,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":269},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":159,"cache_read_input_tokens":86672,"cache_creation_input_tokens":269,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":269,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdejpRgabCEtmoH1jQNBG","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","attributionMcpServer":"Claude Browser","attributionMcpTool":"navigate","type":"assistant","uuid":"88942596-ba80-4b8c-a01c-b1c1189dcac5","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:50:02.850Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"88942596-ba80-4b8c-a01c-b1c1189dcac5","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"c0994d24-7ebc-4999-8662-834f71c61594","agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01GBG6vZgjgsppspEQY1xhUP","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"\"Toggle navigation\\nQUANTCONNECT RESEARCH PUBLICATIONS\\nSTOCK SELECTION STRATEGY BASED ON FUNDAMENTAL FACTORS\\nRESEARCH\\nCOMMUNITY\\n|\\nAbstract\\nIn recent years, factor investing gained significant popularity among global institutional investors. In this tutorial, we first developed a factor selection model to test if factors have the ability to differentiate potential winners and losers in the stock market. Then we use those preselected factors to implement the factor ranking stock selection algorithm based on Factor Based Stock Selection Model for Turkish Equities, 2015, Ayhan Yüksel.\\nFactor Selection\\nQuantConnect provides Morningstar fundamentals data for US Equities. Valuation Ratios is daily data. For others like operation ratios and financial statements data are available for multiple periods depending on the property. To view the fundamental factors that are availalbe, see Data Point Attributes.\\nThe algorithm is designed to test the significance of one factor each time.\\ndef initialize(self):\\n    self.set_start_date(2005,01,01)  #Set Start Date\\n    self.set_end_date(2012,03,01)    #Set End Date\\n    self.set_cash(50000)            #Set Strategy Cash\\n    self.universe_settings.resolution = Resolution.DAILY\\n    self.add_universe(self.coarse_selection_function, self.fine_selection_function)\\n    self.add_equity(\\\"SPY\\\") # add benchmark\\n    self.num_of_course_symbols = 200\\n    self.num_of_portfolio = 5\\n    self._changes = None\\n    self.flag1 = 1  # variable to control the monthly rebalance of coarse and fine selection function\\n    self.flag2 = 0  # variable to control the monthly rebalance of OnData function\\n    self.flag3 = 0  # variable to record the number of rebalancing times\\n        # store the monthly returns of different portfolios in a dataframe\\n    self.df_return = pd.DataFrame(index = range(self.num_of_portfolio+1))\\n        # schedule an event to fire at the first trading day of SPY\\n    self.schedule.on(self.date_rules.month_start(\\\"SPY\\\"), self.time_rules.after_market_open(\\\"SPY\\\"), Action(self.rebalancing))\\n+ Expand\\nStep 1: Ranking the stocks by factor values\\nFirst, we sort the stocks by daily dollar volume and take the top stocks with the highest dollar volumes as our candidates. There is a convenient way using our universe selection API. Universes are refreshed every day by default. Here we use Scheduled events API to trigger code to run at the first trading day each month and use three flag variables to control the rebalancing of CoarseSelection, FineSelection and Ondata functions.\\nCoarse universe selection is the built-in universe data provided by QuantConnect which allows you to filter the universe of over 16,000 symbols to perform rough filtering before your algorithm. Because coarse selection function takes all the Equities including ETFs which have no fundamental data into account, we need the property x.has_fundamental_data to exclude them from our candidate stocks pool.\\n# sort the data by daily dollar volume and take the top entries\\ndef coarse_selection_function(self, coarse):\\n    if self.flag1:\\n        coarse_with_fundamental = [x for x in coarse if x.has_fundamental_data]\\n        sorted_by_volume = sorted(coarse_with_fundamental, key=lambda x: x.dollar_volume, reverse=True)\\n        top = sorted_by_volume[:self.num_of_course_symbols]\\n                return [i.symbol for i in top]\\n    else:\\n        return []\\n+ Expand\\nWe extract the factor values of candidate stocks at the beginning of each month and sort the stocks in ascending order of their factor values. Here we use 12-months' total risk-based capital data\\nx.financial_statements.total_risk_based_capital.twelve_months\\nas an example. It is the sum of Tier 1 and Tier 2 Capital. x.symbol.value can give the string symbol of selected stock x. Then we save those sorted symbols as self.symbol.\\ndef fine_selection_function(self, fine):\\n    if self.flag1:\\n        self.flag1 = 0\\n        self.flag2 = 1\\n        # filter the fine by deleting equities wit zero factor value\\n        filtered_fine = [x for x in fine if x.financial_statements.total_risk_based_capital.twelve_months != 0 ]\\n        # sort the fine by reverse order of factor value\\n        sorted_fine = sorted(filtered_fine, key=lambda x: x.financial_statements.total_risk_based_capital.twelve_months, reverse=True)\\n        self.symbol = [str(x.symbol.value) for x in sorted_fine]\\n        # factor_value = [x.valuation_ratios.pe_ratio for x in sorted_fine]\\n        self.flag3 = self.flag3 + 1\\n        return []\\n    else:\\n        return []\\n+ Expand\\nStep 2: Compute the monthly return of portfolios\\nAt the end of each month, we extract the one-month history close prices of each stock and compute the monthly returns.\\nsorted_symbol = self.symbol\\nself.add_equity(\\\"SPY\\\") # add benchmark\\nfor x in sorted_symbol:\\n    self.add_equity(x)\\nhistory = self.history(20,Resolution.DAILY)\\nmonthly_return =[]\\nnew_symbol_list =[]\\nfor j in range(len(sorted_symbol)):\\n    try:\\n        daily_price = []\\n        for slice in history:\\n            bar = slice[sorted_symbol[j]]\\n            daily_price.append(float(bar.close))\\n        new_symbol_list.append(sorted_symbol[j])\\n        monthly_return.append(daily_price[-1] / daily_price[0] - 1)\\n    except:\\n        self.log(\\\"No history data for \\\" + str(sorted_symbol[j]))\\n        del daily_price\\n# the length of monthly_return list should be divisible by the number of portfolios\\nmonthly_return = monthly_return[:int(math.floor(len(monthly_return) / self.num_of_portfolio) * self.num_of_portfolio)]\\n+ Expand\\nWe divide the stocks into 5 portfolios and compute the average monthly returns of each portfolio. Then we add the monthly return of benchmark \\\"SPY\\\" at the last line of the data frame df_return.\\nreshape_return = np.reshape(monthly_return, (self.num_of_portfolio, len(monthly_return)/self.num_of_portfolio))\\n# calculate the average return of different portfolios\\nport_avg_return = np.mean(reshape_return,axis=1).tolist()\\n# add return of \\\"SPY\\\" as the benchmark  to the end of the return list\\nbenchmark_syl = self.add_equity(\\\"SPY\\\").symbol\\nhistory_benchmark = self.history(20,Resolution.DAILY)\\nbenchmark_daily_price = [float(slice[benchmark_syl].close) for slice in history_benchmark]\\nbenchmark_monthly_return = (benchmark_daily_price[-1]/benchmark_daily_price[0]) - 1\\nport_avg_return.append(benchmark_monthly_return)\\nself.df_return[str(self.flag3)] = port_avg_return\\n+ Expand\\nStep 3: Generate the metrics to test the factor significance\\nAfter getting the monthly returns of portfolios and the benchmark, we compute the average annual return and excess return over benchmark of each portfolio across the whole backtesting period. Then we generate three metrics to judge the significance of each factor.\\nThe first metrics is the correlation between the portfolio' returns and their rank. The absolute value of the correlation coefficient should larger than 0.8.\\nIf the return of the rank first portfolio larger than the portfolio at the bottom of the return rankings, we define it the win po\"\n\n(captured at origin https://quantconnect.com)"},{"type":"text","text":"\n\nTab Context:\n- Executed on tabId: tab-2\n- Available tabs:\n  • tabId tab-2: \"Stock Selection Strategy Based On Fundamental Factors\" (https://quantconnect.com)"}]}]},"uuid":"9be9f30c-2456-4ee4-a933-2bee97ae3b91","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:50:05.099Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"88942596-ba80-4b8c-a01c-b1c1189dcac5","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"9be9f30c-2456-4ee4-a933-2bee97ae3b91","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdejpvXR64wQGoAet64co","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"These articles are particularly valuable because they include complete, working code examples. I'm also noticing a methodological issue here — the factor-significance testing uses quintile portfolios with a fixed 0.8 correlation threshold and doesn't account for multiple comparisons, which directly conflicts with Meridian's pre-registration standards around avoiding in-sample bias and p-hacking.\n\nNow I need to verify the full article catalog through the Learning Center's Articles tab and cross-check against the sitemap's 116 articles across 5 categories. I should also search for ObjectStore and research-to-backtest content in the docs to identify what's missing from their {"parentUuid":"a82553e5-f11f-4852-bcd1-81d506905d6d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"c0994d24-7ebc-4999-8662-834f71c61594","agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01DpyMLVPqpT6MtnZcGoQsnr","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"\"LEARNING CENTER\\nARTICLES\\nQuantConnect maintains collections of related tutorials we call a Learn Series. We have tutorial series covering the topics below - each with a set of 8-10 child tutorials beneath them.\\nInvestment Strategy Library\\nLearn about classic and new investment strategies to incorporate into your portfolio\\nEXPLORE\\nFinancial Python\\nIntroduces basic Python applied to quantitative finance concepts\\nEXPLORE\\nOptions Theory\\nIntroduces options to those who are option novices and have basic knowledge of applied mathematics, statistics and financial markets\\nEXPLORE\\nOptions Applied\\nSimple options trading algorithms on QuantConnect to those who already have basic knowledge of options markets\\nEXPLORE\\nAlternative Data\\nInvestment strategies using Alternative Data to incorporate into your portfolio\\nEXPLORE\\nThe Introduction to Financial Python series is a great place to start because it covers the basics of the Python programming language and how to use it in the context of statistics and finance. The Options Theory series is designed to introduce foundational concepts of Option trading. The Applied Options series then provides examples of implementing popular Options trading strategies with the LEAN trading engine. The Investment Strategy Library contains a diverse collection of tutorials that explain how specific trading stategies work and how to implement them with LEAN.\\nQuantConnect™ 2022. All Rights Reserved\\nTECHNOLOGY\\nAlgorithm Lab\\nDocumentation\\nCommunity\\nTutorials\\nData Library\\nLearning Articles\\nSystem Status\\nCOMPANY\\nAbout\\nAffiliates\\nOur Blog\\nContact\\nPricing\\nIntegration Partners\\nTerms & Conditions\\nPrivacy Policy\"\n\n(captured at origin https://quantconnect.com)"},{"type":"text","text":"\n\nTab Context:\n- Executed on tabId: tab-2\n- Available tabs:\n  • tabId tab-2: \"QuantConnect | Learning Center Articles\" (https://quantconnect.com)"}]}]},"uuid":"00a66f4a-50f3-462e-a283-a39748e2e17b","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:50:26.480Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a82553e5-f11f-4852-bcd1-81d506905d6d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

{"parentUuid":"f4a1ffa8-a39a-419d-b7c1-a56321e10120","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Cdejt4opsBrkFicLuMQYi","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01318DbkxeHh7uSsx8SwSgmt","name":"mcp__Claude_Browser__javascript_tool","input":{"action":"javascript_exec","tabId":"tab-2","text":"(()=>{const t=document.body.innerText.replace(/\\n{2,}/g,'\\n');return JSON.stringify({objstore:t.includes('ObjectStore')||t.includes('object_store'),research:t.includes('Research'),len:t.length,head:t.slice(0,3500)});})()"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1049,"cache_read_input_tokens":91666,"output_tokens":214,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1049},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":214,"cache_read_input_tokens":91666,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1049,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1049,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011Cdejt3gruJx5DCks6nVcz","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","attributionMcpServer":"Claude Browser","attributionMcpTool":"navigate","type":"assistant","uuid":"c7b88edd-1211-42cf-ae61-8143074550e9","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:50:51.298Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"c7b88edd-1211-42cf-ae61-8143074550e9","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"c0994d24-7ebc-4999-8662-834f71c61594","agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01318DbkxeHh7uSsx8SwSgmt","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"\"{\\\"objstore\\\":false,\\\"research\\\":true,\\\"len\\\":9276,\\\"head\\\":\\\"Toggle navigation\\\\nQUANTCONNECT RESEARCH PUBLICATIONS\\\\nGRADIENT BOOSTING MODEL\\\\nRESEARCH\\\\nCOMMUNITY\\\\n|\\\\nAbstract\\\\nIn this tutorial, we train a Gradient Boosting Model (GBM) to forecast the intraday price movements of the SPY ETF using a collection of technical indicators. The implementation is based on the research produced by Zhou et al (2013), where a GBM was found to produce an annualized Sharpe ratio greater than 20. Our research shows that throughout a 5 year backtest, the model underperforms the SPY with its current parameter set. However, we finish the tutorial with highlighting potential areas of further research to improve the model’s performance.\\\\nBackground\\\\nA GBM is trained by setting the initial model prediction to the mean target value in the training set. The model then iteratively builds regression trees to predict the model’s pseudo-residuals on the training set to tighten the fit. The pseudo-residuals are the differences between the target value and the model’s prediction on the current training iteration for each sample. The model’s predictions are made by summing the mean target value and the products of the learning rate and the regression tree outputs. The full algorithm is shown here.\\\\nWe provide technical indicator values as inputs to the GBM. The model is trained to predict the security’s return over the next 10 minutes and the performance of the model’s predictions are assessed using the mean squared error loss function.\\\\nM\\\\nS\\\\nE\\\\n=\\\\nΣ\\\\nn\\\\ni\\\\n=\\\\n1\\\\n(\\\\ny\\\\ni\\\\n−\\\\n^\\\\ny\\\\ni\\\\n)\\\\n2\\\\nn\\\\nM\\\\nS\\\\nE\\\\n=\\\\nΣ\\\\nn\\\\ni\\\\n=\\\\n1\\\\n(\\\\ny\\\\ni\\\\n−\\\\n^\\\\ny\\\\ni\\\\n)\\\\n2\\\\nn\\\\n𝑀\\\\n𝑆\\\\n𝐸\\\\n=\\\\nΣ\\\\n𝑖\\\\n=\\\\n1\\\\n𝑛\\\\n(\\\\n𝑦\\\\n𝑖\\\\n−\\\\n𝑦\\\\n^\\\\n𝑖\\\\n)\\\\n2\\\\n𝑛\\\\nZhou et al (2013) utilize custom loss functions to fit their GBM in a manner that aims to maximize the profit-and-loss or Sharpe ratio over the training data set. The attached notebook shows training the GBM with these custom loss functions leads to poor model predictions.\\\\nMethod\\\\nUniverse Selection\\\\nWe use a ManualUniverseSelectionModel to subscribe to the SPY ETF. The algorithm is designed to work with minute and second data resolutions. In our implementation, we use data on a minute resolution.\\\\nsymbols = [ Symbol.create(\\\\\\\"SPY\\\\\\\", SecurityType.EQUITY, Market.USA) ]\\\\nself.set_universe_selection( ManualUniverseSelectionModel(symbols) )\\\\nself.universe_settings.resolution = Resolution.MINUTE\\\\nAlpha Construction\\\\nThe GradientBoostingAlphaModel predicts the direction of the SPY at each timestep. Each position taken is held for 10 minutes, although this duration is customizable in the constructor. During construction of this Alpha model, we simply set up a dictionary to hold a SymbolData object for each Symbol in the universe. In the case where the universe consists of multiple securities, the Alpha model holds each with equal weighting.\\\\nclass GradientBoostingAlphaModel(AlphaModel):\\\\n    symbol_data_by_symbol = {}\\\\n    def __init__(self, hold_duration = 10):\\\\n        self.hold_duration = hold_duration\\\\n        self.weight = 1\\\\nAlpha Securities Management\\\\nWhen a new security is added to the universe, we create a SymbolData object for it to store information unique to the security. The management of the SymbolData objects occurs in the Alpha model's OnSecuritiesChanged method.\\\\ndef on_securities_changed(self, algorithm, changes):\\\\n    for security in changes.added_securities:\\\\n        symbol = security.symbol\\\\n        self.symbol_data_by_symbol[symbol] = SymbolData(symbol, algorithm, self.hold_duration)\\\\n    for security in changes.removed_securities:\\\\n        symbol_data = self.symbol_data_by_symbol.pop(secu\\\"}\"\n\n(captured at origin https://quantconnect.com)"},{"type":"text","text":"\n\nTab Context:\n- Executed on tabId: tab-2\n- Available tabs:\n  • tabId tab-2: \"Gradient Boosting Model\" (https://quantconnect.com)"}]}]},"uuid":"69c71c37-b8b9-4790-9bca-bd0c7a5777a4","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:50:56.040Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"c7b88edd-1211-42cf-ae61-8143074550e9","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

{"parentUuid":"a6074be9-3c7d-4407-964a-538343ba903d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f3cb034-605e-4d8d-956f-2729a6818e33","agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EChqt9SrdyLQjZCtH9mNNM","type":"tool_result","content":"# Object Store - QuantConnect Documentation\n\n## Summary\nThis documentation page explains QuantConnect's Object Store, a file system for saving, reading, and deleting data across algorithms, research environments, and live trading. It functions as key-value storage supporting strings, JSON, XML, and bytes, with organization-wide access and storage quotas for cloud users.\n\n## Sub-page Links and Titles\n\n1. **Introduction** - Overview of Object Store functionality\n2. **Get All Stored Data** - Iterating through stored keys and values\n3. **Create Sample Data** - Creating dictionaries, strings, bytes, and XML objects\n4. **Save Data** - Methods for persisting strings, JSON, XML, and bytes\n5. **Read Data** - Loading previously stored objects\n6. **Delete Data** - Removing objects from storage\n7. **Cache Data** - Managing data caching behavior\n8. **Get File Path** - Retrieving file paths for Object Store keys\n9. **Storage Quotas** - Understanding cloud storage limits\n10. **Example for DataFrames** - Working with pandas/C# DataFrames\n11. **Example for Plotting** - Visualizing backtest data in research\n12. **Example for Logging** - Recording order data for analysis\n13. **Example of Custom Data** - Handling custom data types\n14. **Preserve Insights Between Deployments** - Cross-deployment data persistence\n15. **Live Trading Considerations** - Live algorithm considerations\n16. **Special Folders** - Reserved folder structures\n\n**Canonical URL:** https://www.quantconnect.com/docs/v2/writing-algorithms/object-store"}]},"uuid":"c384156f-1842-4c4d-9cf8-67c760669a55","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:51:11.538Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a6074be9-3c7d-4407-964a-538343ba903d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

{"parentUuid":"d459c0f8-b539-4257-89af-5f8642e6cb30","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f3cb034-605e-4d8d-956f-2729a6818e33","agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01YSYH5MWhW2xECf24JtcDbg","type":"tool_result","content":"# Applying Research Tutorials & Sub-Pages\n\nBased on the navigation menu, here are the tutorials under \"Applying Research\":\n\n1. **Key Concepts** (`/docs/v2/research-environment/applying-research/key-concepts`) - Introduces the workflow for converting research hypotheses into backtestable strategies.\n\n2. **Mean Reversion** (`/docs/v2/research-environment/applying-research/mean-reversion`) - Covers mean reversion trading strategies.\n\n3. **Random Forest Regression** (`/docs/v2/research-environment/applying-research/random-forest-regression`) - Explores machine learning regression techniques for strategy development.\n\n4. **Uncorrelated Assets** (`/docs/v2/research-environment/applying-research/uncorrelated-assets`) - Addresses portfolio construction using assets with low correlation.\n\n5. **Kalman Filters and Stat Arb** (`/docs/v2/research-environment/applying-research/kalman-filters-and-stat-arb`) - Demonstrates statistical arbitrage methods using Kalman filtering.\n\n6. **PCA and Pairs Trading** (`/docs/v2/research-environment/applying-research/pca-and-pairs-trading`) - Applies principal component analysis to pairs trading strategies.\n\n7. **Hidden Markov Models** (`/docs/v2/research-environment/applying-research/hidden-markov-models`) - Implements HMM techniques for market regime detection.\n\n8. **Long Short-Term Memory** (`/docs/v2/research-environment/applying-research/long-short-term-memory`) - Uses LSTM neural networks for predictive modeling.\n\n9. **Airline Buybacks** (`/docs/v2/research-environment/applying-research/airline-buybacks`) - Case study applying research to airline equity buyback strategies.\n\n10. **Sparse Optimization** (`/docs/v2/research-environment/applying-research/sparse-optimization`) - Optimizes portfolio allocation using sparse techniques."}]},"uuid":"27e6bf85-7aa4-43db-8b6d-6279e0a0ca03","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:51:24.160Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d459c0f8-b539-4257-89af-5f8642e6cb30","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"27e6bf85-7aa4-43db-8b6d-6279e0a0ca03","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"aab31ebc9e886d462","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdejvmY6XFhThBwzfDYkJ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Şimdi yapılandırılmış raporu Türkçe olarak yazacağım, envanter, üç kova, somut reçeteler, katalog ve dokümantasyon arasındaki boşluklar, operatöre kişisel değer ve erişim sınırlamalarını içerecek şekilde. /learning'in bir SPA olduğu gibi temel gerçekleri de dahil edeceğim.\n\nKatalog /api/v2/sharing/learning/courses/list/ ve POST /api/v2/sharing/learning/lessons/read/ aracılığıyla sunuluyor, bu da tekrarlanabilirlik için faydalı. Platform üzerinde 11 ücretsiz kurs ve 3 ücretli Udemy bağlantısı olmak üzere toplam 14 tile var. Boot Camp'te 4 kurs ve 67 etkileşimli görev artı 11 tanıtım videosu bulunuyor; kurs 2'de 5 görev, kurs 3'te 4 etkileşimli görev, kurs 4'te 4 görev (metadata hatalı olsa da), ve kurs 5'te 17 video dersi var.\n\nResearch Fundamentals serisinde 6 kurs ve toplam 50 ders bulunuyor. Ayrıca 5 seri arasında 116 makale URL'si var: ISL 83, Financial Python 14, Options Theory 8, Applied Options 8, Alternative Data 3. BC101 109.058 kayıtlı öğrenci ve 4.0/5 puanı (212 yorum) ile oldukça popüler.\n\nŞimdi üç ana kategoriye bakıyorum. Boot Camp 101'den seçilen dersler arasında evren seçimi, momentum stratejileri, değer hisse senetleri ve diğer teknikler var — her biri belirli QuantConnect API'si özellikleri ve gösterge ısıtması gibi pratik sorunları ele alıyor.\n\nSonra sektör ağırlıklı portföy yönetimi, alternatif veri kaynakları ve fiyat normalizasyonu gibi ileri konulara geçiyorum. Ayrıca OrderEvent akışları, trailing stop'lar, consolidator'lar ve RollingWindow gibi LEAN framework'ün teknik detaylarını inceliyorum — bunların hepsi Meridian'ın gerçek ticaret disiplini için önemli.\n\nAlgorithm Framework'ün beş modülü (UniverseSelection, Alpha, PortfolioConstruction, Risk, Execution) arasındaki ayrımı anlamak gerekiyor. Futures, options ve forex için Boot Camp modülleri var ama Meridian US hisse senetlerine odaklandığı için bunlar düşük öncelik — ancak brokerage modeli ve order yönetimi kısmı hala ilgili.\n\nISL'den iki pratik örnek buldum: biri iki aşamalı evren seçimi ve aylık rebalans ile faktör anlamlılığını test eden tam bir hisse seçim stratejisi, diğeri ise gradient boosting modelini AlphaModel çerçevesinde uygulayan bir örnek. Şimdi metodoloji açısından örtüşen ve çelişen noktaları inceliyorum — overfitting tehlikeleri ve p-hacking gibi istatistiksel tuzaklar var. Gradient-boosting modelinin orijinal makalesi Sharpe oranı 20'den yüksek iddia ediyor, ama QuantConnect'in replikasyonu SPY'ın performansının altında kalıyor ve bunu açıkça gösteriyor — bu bizim kanıtla-iddia-etme ve uydurma yasağı ilkeleriyle tam olarak uyuşuyor. Ancak eksik olan şeyler var: walk-forward analizi, purged/embargoed cross-validation, deflated Sharpe oranı, ön-kayıt kavramı ve bizim research/cards/ disiplininin katalogda karşılığı yok. Ayrıca kill-list ve eşik-sonradan-değiştirme yasağı için bir karşılık da bulunmuyor, ve fundamental faktörlere dayalı hisse seçim stratejisi makalesi kendi içinde çelişkili.\n\nFaktör \"anlamlılığı\" için |ρ|>0.8 gibi sabit bir eşik post-hoc olarak konuluyor, tüm örneklem in-sample, çoklu-karşılaştırma düzeltmesi yok — bu tam olarak C12 L3'ün öğrettiği p-hacking'in kendisi. QC materyali metodolojik olarak tek-otoriter değil; ISL makaleleri kodlama için iyi ama kanıt için değil. Quantopian'dan uyarlanan Quant Lecture Series 2020 öncesi içerik ve ders açıklamaları API'de boş — bakım zayıf.\n\nBoot Camp 101'in 4 dersinde video \"deprecated Legacy Universe\" anlatıyor ve sayfa uyarı koymuş, yani video katmanı bayat. BC102-104 FOREX, Futures, Options kursları Meridian US equities dışında varlık sınıfları. Course 5 Algorithmic Trading A-Z üçüncü taraf giriş seviyesi video, yeni bilgi yok. C8 Python/NumPy/pandas/matplotlib girişi, C10 istatistik ve veri analizi temelleri — bunlar alakasız.\n\nOptions Theory ve Applied Options makaleleri opsiyon dışı, Introduction to Financial Python temel seviye. ISL'nin çoğu makalesi strateji-fikri olup Meridian'ın kendi öğrenme döngüsü var; fikir kaynağı olarak düşük öncelik ama bazıları uygulama reçetesi taşıyor. 3 ücretli Udemy kursu dış platform ve giriş seviyesi.\n\nKatalogta olmayan ama docs'ta var: ObjectStore'un /docs/v2/writing-algorithms bölümü işe yarar.\n\nObjectStore'un 16 alt sayfası var — \"Preserve Insights Between Deployments\", \"Example for DataFrames\", \"Storage Quotas\" gibi — katalogda hiç geçmiyor. Research'ten backtest'e köprü kuran /docs/v2/research-environment/applying-research/key-concepts ve 9 tutorial var. Meta Analysis bölümü backtest, optimization ve live sonuç analizi için — bu karne ve ölçüm iş akışımıza en yakın. Custom data sadece Course 5 L9'da video olarak mevcut.\n\nOperatöre kişisel değer açısından sertifika yok, kurs sayfalarında \"certificate\" yazıyor.\n\nSertifika yok, sadece ilerleme yüzdesi, \"Completed\" sekmesi ve rating var — CV değeri yok. BC101 yaklaşık 6-8 saat sürüyor (56 görev + 11 video ≈ 90 dakika) ve ücretsiz. Eğer QC'ye canlı dağıtım düşünülüyorsa BC101'in L1-L2, L5 ve L8 (LEAN idiomları, order events, framework) yeterli; gerisi atlanabilir. Görev içeriği ve çözüm kodu enroll olmadan okunamıyor — ben enroll olmadım hesap durumunu değiştirmemek için.\n\nLearning sayfası JavaScript ile render ediliyor; katalog API'si kurs listesini döndürüyor ve ders bilgileri ayrı bir endpoint'ten geliyor. Görev metinleri ve çözümler enrollment arkasında kaldığı için okuyamadım. Bazı kurs ID'leri (6, 7, 9) silinmiş veya gizli. Şimdi API adlarının doğruluğuna dikkat ederek somut örnekler yazmalıyım — modern Python API'si snake_case kullanıyor, örneğin resolution ayarlamak için `self.universe_settings.resolution` gibi.\n\nDefining API calls...\n\nI'm distinguishing between code I've verified directly from the source material versus patterns inferred from lesson titles, making sure to flag what's actually documented versus what I'm inferring. Now I need to write up the findings, keeping it clear and organized.
